For the colonial era, see <a href="/wiki/Danish_overseas_colonies" title="Danish overseas colonies">Danish overseas colonies</a>.   </li><li style="line-height: inherit; margin: 0"> <a href="/wiki/%2B298" class="mw-redirect" title="+298">+298</a><span class="nowrap">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;</span>(<a href="/wiki/Telephone_numbers_in_the_Faroe_Islands" title="Telephone numbers in the Faroe Islands">Faroe Islands</a>)
  </li><li style="line-height: inherit; margin: 0"> <a href="/wiki/%2B299" class="mw-redirect" title="+299">+299</a><span class="nowrap">&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;</span>(<a href="/wiki/Telephone_numbers_in_Greenland" title="Telephone numbers in Greenland">Greenland</a>)
 </li></ul>
</div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/ISO_3166" title="ISO 3166">ISO 3166 code</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/ISO_3166-2:DK" title="ISO 3166-2:DK">DK</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Country_code_top-level_domain" title="Country code top-level domain">Internet TLD</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><div class="collapsible-list mw-collapsible mw-collapsed" style="text-align: left;">
<div style="line-height: 1.6em; font-weight: bold; background:transparent;text-align:left;font-weight:normal;"><div>3 TLDs</div></div>
<ul class="mw-collapsible-content" style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; line-height: inherit; list-style: none; margin-left: 0;"><li style="line-height: inherit; margin: 0"> <a href="/wiki/.dk" title=".dk">.dk</a><span class="nowrap">&#160;&#160;</span>(Denmark)<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;e&#93;</a></sup>
  </li><li style="line-height: inherit; margin: 0"> <a href="/wiki/.fo" title=".fo">.fo</a><span class="nowrap">&#160;&#160;</span>(Faroe Islands)
  </li><li style="line-height: inherit; margin: 0"> <a href="/wiki/.gl" title=".gl">.gl</a><span class="nowrap">&#160;&#160;</span>(Greenland)
 </li></ul>
</div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>The <b>Danish Realm</b> (<a href="/wiki/Danish_language" title="Danish language">Danish</a>: <i lang="da">Danmarks Rige</i>; <a href="/wiki/Faroese_language" title="Faroese language">Faroese</a>: <i lang="fo">Danmarkar Ríki</i>; <a href="/wiki/Greenlandic_language" title="Greenlandic language">Greenlandic</a>: <i lang="kl">Danmarkip Naalagaaffik</i>),<sup id="cite_ref-Danmarkip_Naalagaaffik_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Danmarkip_Naalagaaffik-19">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> officially the <b>Kingdom of Denmark</b> (Danish: <i lang="da">Kongeriget Danmark</i>;<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;f&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Faroese: <i lang="fo">Kongsríki Danmarkar</i>;<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> Greenlandic: <i lang="kl">Kunngeqarfik Danmarki</i>), is a <a href="/wiki/Sovereign_state" title="Sovereign state">sovereign state</a> located in <a href="/wiki/Northern_Europe" title="Northern Europe">Northern Europe</a> and <a href="/wiki/North_America" title="North America">North America</a>. It consists of metropolitan <a href="/wiki/Denmark" title="Denmark">Denmark</a>—the kingdom's territory in <a href="/wiki/Continental_Europe" title="Continental Europe">continental Europe</a> and sometimes called "Denmark proper" (Danish: <i lang="da">egentlige Danmark</i>)—and the realm's two autonomous regions: the <a href="/wiki/Faroe_Islands" title="Faroe Islands">Faroe Islands</a> and <a href="/wiki/Greenland" title="Greenland">Greenland</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Administrative_divisions_–_Denmark_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Administrative_divisions_–_Denmark-23">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> The relationship between the three parts of the Kingdom is also known as <b>The unity of the Realm</b> (Danish: <i lang="da">Rigsfællesskabet</i>;<sup id="cite_ref-:14_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:14-24">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> Faroese: <i lang="fo">ríkisfelagsskapurin</i>;<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> Greenlandic: <i lang="kl">naalagaaffeqatigiinneq</i>).<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Kingdom of Denmark is not a <a href="/wiki/Federation" title="Federation">federation</a>; similar to the <a href="/wiki/Realm_of_New_Zealand" title="Realm of New Zealand">Realm of New Zealand</a>, it is a concept encompassing the three autonomous legal systems of Denmark, the Faroe Islands and Greenland, united under <a href="/wiki/Monarchy_of_Denmark" title="Monarchy of Denmark">its monarch</a>. The Kingdom of Denmark is a <a href="/wiki/Unitary_state" title="Unitary state">unitary</a> sovereign state. It has <a href="/wiki/Territorial_claims_in_the_Arctic#Denmark" title="Territorial claims in the Arctic">Arctic territorial claims</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Arctic_Ocean" title="Arctic Ocean">Arctic Ocean</a>: various sites near the <a href="/wiki/North_Pole" title="North Pole">North Pole</a> (<a href="/wiki/Lomonosov_Ridge" title="Lomonosov Ridge">Lomonosov Ridge</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gakkel_Ridge" title="Gakkel Ridge">Gakkel Ridge</a>, <a href="/wiki/Alpha_Ridge" title="Alpha Ridge">Alpha</a>-<a href="/wiki/Mendeleev_Ridge" title="Mendeleev Ridge">Mendeleev Ridge</a> complex, and the Chukchi Borderland). Constitutionally, the Kingdom of Denmark encompasses the realm, but the Faroe Islands and Greenland have an extended degree of autonomy to govern their relations.
</p><p>The Faroe Islands and Greenland have been under the Crown of Denmark since 1397 (<i><a href="/wiki/De_facto" title="De facto">de facto</a></i>) when the <a href="/wiki/Kalmar_Union" title="Kalmar Union">Kalmar Union</a> was ratified, and part of the Danish Realm since 1814 (<i><a href="/wiki/De_jure" title="De jure">de jure</a></i>). However, due to their separate historical and cultural identities, these parts of the Realm now have an extensive degree of self-government and have assumed <a href="/wiki/Legislature" title="Legislature">legislative</a> and <a href="/wiki/Executive_(government)" title="Executive (government)">administrative</a> responsibility in a substantial number of fields.<sup id="cite_ref-denmark.dk_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-denmark.dk-27">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Legal matters in the Realm are subject to the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Denmark" title="Constitution of Denmark">Constitution of the Realm of Denmark</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> It stipulates that it applies for all parts of the Kingdom of Denmark and that legislative, executive and judicial powers are the responsibility of the <a href="/wiki/Folketing" title="Folketing">Parliament of the Kingdom of Denmark</a> (Danish: <i lang="da">Folketing</i>), the <a href="/wiki/Cabinet_of_Denmark" title="Cabinet of Denmark">Government of Denmark</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Court_(Denmark)" title="Supreme Court (Denmark)">Supreme Court of Denmark</a>. The Faroe Islands were <a href="/wiki/1946_Faroese_independence_referendum" title="1946 Faroese independence referendum">granted home rule</a> via an <a href="/wiki/Independence_referendum" title="Independence referendum">independence referendum</a> in 1946, and Greenland did so in a <a href="/wiki/1979_Greenlandic_home_rule_referendum" title="1979 Greenlandic home rule referendum">1979 referendum</a>. In 2005, the Faroes received a self-government arrangement, and in 2009 Greenland received "<a href="#Terminology">self rule</a>", thus leaving the government of Denmark with little influence over the matters of internal affairs that are <a href="/wiki/Devolution" title="Devolution">devolved</a> to the local governments of Greenland and the Faroe Islands.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Name">Name</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Danish_Realm&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Name">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Sovereign_state" title="Sovereign state">sovereign state</a> that holds <a href="/wiki/Sovereignty" title="Sovereignty">sovereignty</a> over the Realm of Denmark (Danish: <i lang="da">Danmarks Rige</i>) is named "Kingdom of Denmark" (Danish: <i lang="da">Kongeriget Danmark</i>).<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:11_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:11-30">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Denmark" title="Constitution of Denmark">Constitution of the Kingdom of Denmark</a> refers to the state's territory as <span title="Danish-language text"><i lang="da">Danmarks Rige</i></span>, which means "The Realm of Denmark".<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Danish term <span title="Danish-language text"><i lang="da">rigsfællesskabet</i></span>, translated as "The unity of the Realm",<sup id="cite_ref-:14_24-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:14-24">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> the "commonwealth of the Realm",<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> or the "Danish Commonwealth"<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> refers to the <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_law" title="Constitutional law">constitutional status</a> of the relationship between Denmark, the Faroe Islands, and Greenland.<sup id="cite_ref-:15_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:15-35">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> The name was used by Danish and Greenlandic authorities in the negotiations for home rule introduced in 1979, and has become popular since the beginning of the 1990s. The acts establishing the 1948 Faroese home rule and the 1979 Greenlandic home rule use the term <i>rigsenheden</i> instead. Jurist <a href="/wiki/Frederik_Harhoff" title="Frederik Harhoff">Frederik Harhoff</a> argued in 1993 that <i>rigsenheden</i> should be replaced with <i>rigsfællesskabet</i>, as the former implies a common identity, while the latter implied a community of different identities.<sup id="cite_ref-:15_35-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:15-35">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> The use of the expression <i>Rigsfællesskabet</i> though can be traced back to at least 1908.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Population_and_area">Population and area</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Danish_Realm&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Population and area">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Denmark" title="Demographics of Denmark">Demographics of Denmark</a>, <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="Demographics of the Faroe Islands">Demographics of the Faroe Islands</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Greenland" title="Demographics of Greenland">Demographics of Greenland</a></div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Denmark" title="Geography of Denmark">Geography of Denmark</a>, <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="Geography of the Faroe Islands">Geography of the Faroe Islands</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Greenland" title="Geography of Greenland">Geography of Greenland</a></div>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kingdom_of_Denmark,_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_colored_(zoom).svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e8/Kingdom_of_Denmark%2C_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_colored_%28zoom%29.svg/300px-Kingdom_of_Denmark%2C_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_colored_%28zoom%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="300" height="213" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e8/Kingdom_of_Denmark%2C_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_colored_%28zoom%29.svg/450px-Kingdom_of_Denmark%2C_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_colored_%28zoom%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e8/Kingdom_of_Denmark%2C_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_colored_%28zoom%29.svg/600px-Kingdom_of_Denmark%2C_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_colored_%28zoom%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="485" data-file-height="345" /></a><figcaption>Comparison map: Greenland, the Faroe Islands (enlarged) and Denmark differ significantly in size. The Danish Realm is spread far apart, across the North Atlantic Ocean and <a href="/wiki/North_Sea" title="North Sea">North Sea</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Denmark's population is by far the largest of the three; 5.8 million people live in Denmark, and about 52,000 and 56,000 in the Faroe Island and Greenland, respectively.<sup id="cite_ref-:4_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-37">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:5_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:5-38">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:6_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:6-39">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> In comparison, there are ten cities in Denmark with a population above 50,000 people.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> Denmark is populated by the <a href="/wiki/Danes" title="Danes">Danes</a>, the Faroe Island by the <a href="/wiki/Faroe_Islanders" title="Faroe Islanders">Faroese</a>, and Greenland by the <a href="/wiki/Greenlandic_Inuit" title="Greenlandic Inuit">Greenlandic Inuit</a>. In both the Faroe Islands and Greenland, Danes make up 7.6% of the population, as of 2018<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Danish_Realm&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>.<sup id="cite_ref-:8_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:8-41">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:9_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:9-42">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:10_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:10-43">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> As of 2020<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Danish_Realm&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, there are about 11,000 Faeroese-born and 17,000 Greenlandic-born people living in Denmark.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>With respect to area, Greenland is by far the largest, and makes up 98% of the realm.<sup id="cite_ref-:7_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:7-46">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> The entire kingdom has an area of 2.2&#160;million square kilometres (0.85&#160;million square miles), and is according to <i><a href="/wiki/The_World_Factbook" title="The World Factbook">The World Factbook</a></i> the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_area" title="List of countries and dependencies by area">twelfth largest country</a> in the world, the same rank held by Greenland alone. Denmark alone has an area of about 43,000&#160;km<sup>2</sup>, and is no. 133 on that list.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Denmark is situated in <a href="/wiki/Northern_Europe" title="Northern Europe">Northern Europe</a> and is flat and arable, the Faroe Islands in the <a href="/wiki/Northern_Atlantic" class="mw-redirect" title="Northern Atlantic">Northern Atlantic</a> and are rugged with cliffs along the coast, while Greenland is in the North Atlantic and <a href="/wiki/Arctic" title="Arctic">Arctic</a>, and is 79% <a href="/wiki/Greenland_ice_sheet" title="Greenland ice sheet">covered in ice</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:8_41-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:8-41">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:9_42-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:9-42">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:10_43-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:10-43">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> Greenland is the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_population_density" title="List of countries and dependencies by population density">most sparsely populated territory</a> in the world, according to the <a href="/wiki/World_Bank_Group" title="World Bank Group">World Bank</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<p>The Kingdom has submitted <a href="/wiki/Territorial_claims_in_the_Arctic" title="Territorial claims in the Arctic">five claims</a> to the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> that its <a href="/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone" title="Exclusive economic zone">exclusive economic zone</a> extends beyond the usual 200 <a href="/wiki/Nautical_mile" title="Nautical mile">nautical miles</a> limit: one north and one south of the Faroe Islands, and three around Greenland. One Greenlandic claim includes the <a href="/wiki/North_Pole" title="North Pole">North Pole</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Lomonosov_Ridge" title="Lomonosov Ridge">Lomonosov Ridge</a>, and extend all the way to the Russian exclusive economic zone.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> Claims overlapping with other nations' claims have to be resolved through negotiation;<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> in 2019, <a href="/wiki/Iceland" title="Iceland">Iceland</a>, <a href="/wiki/Norway" title="Norway">Norway</a> and the Kingdom of Denmark settled their claims to the area north of the Faroe Islands.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Kingdom was in a <a href="/wiki/Whisky_War" title="Whisky War">dispute with Canada</a> on who has sovereignty over <a href="/wiki/Hans_Island" title="Hans Island">Hans Island</a><sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> between 1978 and 2022. The two governments eventually settled on a border running approximately halfway through the island.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Historical_background">Historical background</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Danish_Realm&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Historical background">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/History_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="History of the Faroe Islands">History of the Faroe Islands</a>, <a href="/wiki/History_of_Greenland" title="History of Greenland">History of Greenland</a>, and <a href="/wiki/History_of_Iceland" title="History of Iceland">History of Iceland</a></div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Faroese_independence_movement" title="Faroese independence movement">Faroese independence movement</a> and <a href="/wiki/Greenlandic_independence" title="Greenlandic independence">Greenlandic independence</a></div>
<p>The Faroe Islands were settled by Norwegian Vikings in the 9th century, displacing Irish monks already there.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-55">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Iceland was settled in the 9th century by <a href="/wiki/Norsemen" title="Norsemen">Norsemen</a>, and was <a href="/wiki/Icelandic_Commonwealth" title="Icelandic Commonwealth">a free state</a> until 1262/1264, when it came under Norwegian taxation.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-56">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> Greenland, already populated by the indigenous <a href="/wiki/Greenlandic_Inuit" title="Greenlandic Inuit">Greenlandic Inuit</a>, was settled by Norwegians in the 10th century, among those <a href="/wiki/Erik_the_Red" title="Erik the Red">Erik the Red</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-57">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> The connection to Greenland was lost in the 15th century, but <a href="/wiki/Denmark%E2%80%93Norway" title="Denmark–Norway">Denmark–Norway</a> again established connections in 1721 though the missionary <a href="/wiki/Hans_Egede" title="Hans Egede">Hans Egede</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_57-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-57">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1814, Denmark ceded Norway to Sweden under the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Kiel" title="Treaty of Kiel">Treaty of Kiel</a>, but kept control of the Faroe Islands, Greenland, and Iceland.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> The colonies on Greenland were situated on the west coast, and as a condition for the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_the_Danish_West_Indies" title="Treaty of the Danish West Indies">sale of the Danish West Indies</a> to the United States in 1917, the U.S. recognised Danish sovereignty over the whole island, and most countries followed suit. One exception was Norway who in 1931 <a href="/wiki/Erik_the_Red%27s_Land" title="Erik the Red&#39;s Land">occupied parts of East Greenland</a>, but abandoned their claim in 1933, when it lost the case at the <a href="/wiki/Permanent_Court_of_International_Justice" title="Permanent Court of International Justice">Permanent Court of International Justice</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_57-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-57">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In Iceland there were a <a href="/wiki/Icelandic_independence_movement" title="Icelandic independence movement">growing nationalism</a> in the 19th century, and Iceland was in 1874 given its own constitution and increased autonomy, but still with the <a href="/wiki/Executive_(government)" title="Executive (government)">executive power</a> in Danish hands. Iceland was granted home rule in 1904, and, by the <a href="/wiki/Danish%E2%80%93Icelandic_Act_of_Union" title="Danish–Icelandic Act of Union">Danish–Icelandic Act of Union</a>, full independence in 1918. The act established a personal union between Denmark and the newly created <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Iceland" title="Kingdom of Iceland">Kingdom of Iceland</a>, with Denmark handling coastal protection and foreign affairs. In 1944, Iceland abolished the personal union and adopted a new constitution that established the <a href="/wiki/Iceland" title="Iceland">current republic</a>, after <a href="/wiki/1944_Icelandic_constitutional_referendum" title="1944 Icelandic constitutional referendum">a referendum</a> on the subject. This happened during World War II, where Denmark and Iceland were cut off from each other, as <a href="/wiki/Denmark_in_World_War_II" title="Denmark in World War II">Denmark was occupied by Germany</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Occupation_of_Iceland#United_States_occupation" class="mw-redirect" title="Occupation of Iceland">Iceland by the United States</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_56-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-56">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Faroe Islands were made a Danish <a href="/wiki/Amt" title="Amt">county</a> in 1816, and with the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Denmark" title="Constitution of Denmark">constitution of 1849</a>, it gained representation in the <a href="/wiki/Rigsdagen" title="Rigsdagen">Rigsdag</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_55-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-55">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> During World War II, the Faroe Islands were <a href="/wiki/British_occupation_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="British occupation of the Faroe Islands">occupied by the United Kingdom</a> and they largely administered themselves.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_55-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-55">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> After the war, it was clear that the old system could not be reinstated.<sup id="cite_ref-:3_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-59">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:0_55-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-55">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> In <a href="/wiki/1946_Faroese_independence_referendum" title="1946 Faroese independence referendum">an independence referendum</a> in 1946, 50.7% of the Faeroese voted for independence,<sup id="cite_ref-:3_59-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-59">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> but the result was rejected by the Danish government.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_55-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-55">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Instead, after negotiations between the Faroe Islands and Denmark, the Faroe Islands were granted "home rule" in 1948.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_55-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-55">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:3_59-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-59">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Greenland was originally administered as two separate colonies, viz. <a href="/wiki/North_Greenland" title="North Greenland">North</a> and <a href="/wiki/South_Greenland" title="South Greenland">South Greenland</a>. In 1950, these two were merged as the <a href="/wiki/Colony_of_Greenland" title="Colony of Greenland">Colony of Greenland</a>. Following the <a href="/wiki/1953_Danish_constitutional_and_electoral_age_referendum" title="1953 Danish constitutional and electoral age referendum">constitutional reform in 1953</a>, Greenland was <a href="/wiki/County_of_Greenland" class="mw-redirect" title="County of Greenland">incorporated into Denmark as a county</a> and given representation in the <a href="/wiki/Folketing" title="Folketing">Folketing</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> When Denmark joined the <a href="/wiki/European_Communities" title="European Communities">European Communities</a> (EC) in 1972, Greenland followed, despite 70% of the Greenlandic voters voting against it in <a href="/wiki/1972_Danish_European_Communities_membership_referendum" title="1972 Danish European Communities membership referendum">the referendum</a>. As a home rule agreement would allow them to leave again (the <a href="/wiki/Faroe_Islands_and_the_EU" class="mw-redirect" title="Faroe Islands and the EU">Faroe Islands did not join the EC</a>), this was an important factor in the increasing support for home rule. Another factor was a desire to make Greenland <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Greenland" title="Languages of Greenland">more Greenlandic</a> and <a href="/wiki/Danish_people_in_Greenland#Danish_language" title="Danish people in Greenland">less Danish</a>. They were given home rule in 1979 and <a href="/wiki/Withdrawal_of_Greenland_from_the_European_Communities" title="Withdrawal of Greenland from the European Communities">left the EC</a> in 1985. Under the home rule agreement, Greenland gradually took over more responsibility from the Danish state. In 2009, the home rule was replaced with "self rule", granting greater autonomy.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_57-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-57">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Constitutional_status">Constitutional status</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Danish_Realm&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Constitutional status">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Denmark" title="Constitution of Denmark">Danish constitution</a> also applies in the Faroe Islands and Greenland, as section one states that it "shall apply to all parts of the Kingdom of Denmark".<sup id="cite_ref-:11_30-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:11-30">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> The sovereignty of the Faroe Islands and Greenland is held by the Danish state. The Kingdom of Denmark is a <a href="/wiki/Unitary_state" title="Unitary state">unitary state</a>,<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> with the <a href="/wiki/Folketing" title="Folketing">Folketing</a> being its unicameral legislature. The Faroe Islands and Greenland each elect two members to the parliament; the remaining 175 members are elected in Denmark.<sup id="cite_ref-:11_30-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:11-30">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Home_rule_and_self_rule">Home rule and self rule</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Danish_Realm&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Home rule and self rule">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Folketing have by law given the Faroe Islands and Greenland extensive autonomy; the Faroe Islands were given "home rule" in 1948, and Greenland in 1979. Greenland's home rule was replaced in 2009 by "self rule".<sup id="cite_ref-:0_55-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-55">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:2_57-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-57">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> There is an ongoing legal debate about what constitutional weight these arrangements have. In general, there are two conflicting views: (a) the laws delegate power from the Folketing and can be revoked unilaterally by it, and (b) the laws have special status so changes require the consent of the Faeroese <a href="/wiki/L%C3%B8gting" title="Løgting">Løgting</a> or the Greenlandic <a href="/wiki/Inatsisartut" title="Inatsisartut">Inatsisartut</a>, respectively.<sup id="cite_ref-:3_59-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-59">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:12_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:12-61">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Proponents of the first interpretation include <a href="/wiki/Alf_Ross" title="Alf Ross">Alf Ross</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-:3_59-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-59">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> Poul Meyer<sup id="cite_ref-:3_59-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-59">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Jens_Peter_Christensen" title="Jens Peter Christensen">Jens Peter Christensen</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:13_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:13-62">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> Ross, the chief architect of the Faeroese home rule, compared it to an extended version of the autonomy of municipalities.<sup id="cite_ref-:3_59-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-59">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> Meyer wrote in 1947, prior to the Faeroese home rule, that if power was delegated as extensive in other parts of the country, it would probably breach section 2 of the 1915 constitution, suggesting it did not do that here due to the Faroe Islands' separate history.<sup id="cite_ref-:3_59-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-59">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> Similarly, Christensen, a <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Court_(Denmark)" title="Supreme Court (Denmark)">Supreme Court</a> judge, said that due to the special circumstances, the scope of delegation need not be strictly defined.<sup id="cite_ref-:13_62-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:13-62">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Proponents of the second interpretation include Edward Mitens,<sup id="cite_ref-:3_59-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-59">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Max_S%C3%B8rensen" title="Max Sørensen">Max Sørensen</a><sup id="cite_ref-:12_61-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:12-61">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Frederik_Harhoff" title="Frederik Harhoff">Frederik Harhoff</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:3_59-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-59">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> Mitens, a Faeroese jurist and politician, argued that the Faeroese home rule had been approved by both the Løgting and the <a href="/wiki/Rigsdagen" title="Rigsdagen">Rigsdag</a>, so it was an agreement between two parties, in particular because the approval by the Løgting happened according to special rules put in place in 1940 with the consent of the Danish representative there, during the occupation by the United Kingdom.<sup id="cite_ref-:3_59-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-59">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> Sørensen said the intention with the Faeroese home rule was that it should not be unilaterally changed, as stated in the preamble, so it had that effect.<sup id="cite_ref-:12_61-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:12-61">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> Harhoff, in his 1993 <a href="/wiki/Doctorate" title="Doctorate">Doctorate</a> dissertation, considered the home rule acts of the Faroe Islands and Greenland to be somewhere in between the constitution and a usual act by the Folketing, as it had <a href="/wiki/Customary_law" title="Customary law">been treated as such</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:3_59-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-59">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Greenlandic_independence">Greenlandic independence</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Danish_Realm&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Greenlandic independence">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Greenlandic_independence" title="Greenlandic independence">Greenlandic independence</a></div>
<p>The Greenlandic self rule act of 2009 gives Greenland a way to achieve independence. First, the Greenlandic people must make the decision, after which there should be negotiations between the Greenlandic government (<i><a href="/wiki/Naalakkersuisut" title="Naalakkersuisut">Naalakkersuisut</a></i>) and the Danish government about how to practically implement it. The agreement reached needs to be ratified by Inatsisartut, and approved in a referendum in Greenland. It also needs consent from the Folketing, in accordance with section 19 of the Danish constitution.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> That section states that any changes to the Kingdom's territory needs to be approved by the Folketing.<sup id="cite_ref-:11_30-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:11-30">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> Greenlandic independence does not require a constitutional change; instead, should Greenland become independent, the rules in the constitution regarding Greenland becomes void.<sup id="cite_ref-:16_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:16-64">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>With regards to <a href="/wiki/International_law" title="International law">international law</a>, Denmark signed the <a href="/wiki/Indigenous_and_Tribal_Peoples_Convention,_1989" title="Indigenous and Tribal Peoples Convention, 1989">Indigenous and Tribal Peoples Convention</a> in 1996 and acknowledged the <a href="/wiki/Greenlandic_Inuit" title="Greenlandic Inuit">Greenlandic Inuit</a> as an <a href="/wiki/Indigenous_peoples" title="Indigenous peoples">indigenous people</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:16_64-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:16-64">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:12_61-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:12-61">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> In the 2009 self rule act, Denmark recognised the Greenlandic people as a "people" within the context of international law, and their inherent right to <a href="/wiki/Self-determination" title="Self-determination">self-determination</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_57-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-57">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Devolved_powers">Devolved powers</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Danish_Realm&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Devolved powers">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Home_rule" title="Home rule">Home rule</a>, <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="Politics of the Faroe Islands">Politics of the Faroe Islands</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Greenland" title="Politics of Greenland">Politics of Greenland</a></div>
<p>The Kingdom of Denmark constitutes a unified sovereign state, with equal status between its constituent parts.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Devolution" title="Devolution">Devolution</a> differs from <a href="/wiki/Federalism" title="Federalism">federalism</a> in that the devolved powers of the subnational authority ultimately reside in central government, thus the state remains <i><a href="/wiki/De_jure" title="De jure">de jure</a></i> unitary.
</p><p>The Self-Government Arrangements devolves political competence and responsibility from the Danish political authorities to the Faroese and the Greenlandic political authorities. The Faroese and Greenlandic authorities administer the tasks taken over from the state, enact legislation in these specific fields and have the economic responsibility for solving these tasks. The <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Denmark" title="Politics of Denmark">Danish government</a> provides an annual grant to the Faroese and the Greenlandic authorities to cover the costs of these devolved areas.<sup id="cite_ref-denmark.dk_27-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-denmark.dk-27">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tinganes.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e7/Tinganes.jpg/220px-Tinganes.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e7/Tinganes.jpg/330px-Tinganes.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e7/Tinganes.jpg/440px-Tinganes.jpg 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="426" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Tinganes" title="Tinganes">Tinganes</a>, in the capital <a href="/wiki/T%C3%B3rshavn" title="Tórshavn">Tórshavn</a>, is the location of the Faroese Home Government.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The 1948 "Home Rule Act of the Faroe Islands" sets out the terms of Faroese <a href="#Terminology">home rule</a>. The Act states, "...the Faroe Islands shall constitute a <a href="/wiki/Self-governance" title="Self-governance">self-governing</a> community within the State of Denmark."<sup id="cite_ref-Faroese_home_rule_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Faroese_home_rule-8">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> It establishes the home government of the Faroe Islands (<i>Landsstýrið</i>) and the Faroese parliament, the <i><a href="/wiki/L%C3%B8gting" title="Løgting">Løgting</a></i>. More significantly, the Act specifies the powers devolved from the Government of Denmark, including: <a href="/wiki/Local_government" title="Local government">local government</a> and municipal affairs; <a href="/wiki/Taxation" class="mw-redirect" title="Taxation">taxation</a>, at a local and territorial level; <a href="/wiki/Public_service" title="Public service">public services</a>, including police and town planning; <a href="/wiki/Welfare_state" title="Welfare state">welfare services</a>, such as housing; primary and secondary education; Archives, libraries, museums; <a href="/wiki/Agriculture" title="Agriculture">agriculture</a> and fishing; entertainment; among other areas.<sup id="cite_ref-Faroese_home_rule_8-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Faroese_home_rule-8">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> The Faroe Islands were previously administered as a <a href="/wiki/Counties_of_Denmark" title="Counties of Denmark">Danish county</a> (<i>amt</i>); the Home Rule Act abolished the post of <i>Amtmand</i> (County Governor) and replaced it with the role of <i>Rigsombudsmand</i> (<a href="/wiki/High_Commission_of_Denmark_in_the_Faroe_Islands#The_High_Commissioner_of_the_Faroe_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="High Commission of Denmark in the Faroe Islands">High Commissioner of the Danish government</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-Faroese_home_rule_8-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Faroese_home_rule-8">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> These powers were expanded in a 2005 Act, which named the Faroese home government as an "equal partner" with the Danish government.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 1978 "Greenland Home Rule Act" devolves powers in much the same way as the Faroese Home Rule Act. It sets out a <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Greenland" class="mw-redirect" title="Government of Greenland">home rule government</a> and <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Greenland" class="mw-redirect" title="Parliament of Greenland">Greenlandic parliament</a>. Specific areas of governance specified in the act include: Organization of local government; Fishing and agriculture; Welfare system; <a href="/wiki/Environmental_protection" title="Environmental protection">protection of the environment</a>; other areas affecting Greenlanders directly, etc.<sup id="cite_ref-Greenland_9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Greenland-9">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 21 June 2009, Greenland assumed <a href="/wiki/Self-determination" title="Self-determination">self-determination</a> with responsibility for self-government of <a href="/wiki/Judiciary" title="Judiciary">judicial</a> affairs, policing, natural resources, immigration and border controls. Also, Greenlanders were recognised as a <a href="/wiki/Self-determination#Defining_&quot;peoples&quot;" title="Self-determination">separate people</a> under international law.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> Greenland is now described as having "<a href="#Terminology">self rule</a>", with its home government exercising a wider range of powers.
</p><p>There are a number of matters that can not be acquired by the territories; <a href="/wiki/Constitution" title="Constitution">Constitutional</a> affairs, foreign policy, <a href="/wiki/Danish_Defence" title="Danish Defence">defence</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Denmark" class="mw-redirect" title="Supreme Court of Denmark">Supreme Court</a>, <a href="/wiki/Citizenship" title="Citizenship">citizenship</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Monetary_policy" title="Monetary policy">monetary policy</a>. Additionally, the Faroese and Greenlandic parliaments are subordinate to the <a href="/wiki/Folketing" title="Folketing">Danish parliament</a>, where the two territories are represented by two seats each (from a total of 179 seats).
</p><p>The Faroe Islands have gradually taken control of more and more areas of responsibility according to their Home Rule Act from 1948.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> The Faroese/Danish act of 2005 states: "This law is based on an agreement between the Governments of the Faroe Islands and Denmark as equal partners."<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_affairs">Foreign affairs</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Danish_Realm&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Foreign affairs">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Denmark" title="Foreign relations of Denmark">Foreign relations of Denmark</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Also related: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="Foreign relations of the Faroe Islands">Foreign relations of the Faroe Islands</a> and <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Greenland" title="Foreign relations of Greenland">Foreign relations of Greenland</a></div>
<p>Previously, most <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Denmark" title="Foreign relations of Denmark">foreign relations</a> were undertaken exclusively by the Government of Denmark on behalf of the entire realm, but more recently the Faroe Islands and Greenland have increased their role in foreign policy. Representatives for both have joined Danish delegations in discussions on some international matters, such as fishing rights. Greenlandic representatives were included in the process of a new treaty between Denmark and the US regarding the <a href="/wiki/Pituffik_Space_Base" title="Pituffik Space Base">Pituffik Space Base</a> in northwest Greenland.
</p><p>The Kingdom of Denmark as a whole is a member of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>, <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a>, the <a href="/wiki/OECD" title="OECD">OECD</a> and the <a href="/wiki/World_Trade_Organization" title="World Trade Organization">World Trade Organization</a>. The Faroe Islands and Greenland are associated members of the <a href="/wiki/Nordic_Council" title="Nordic Council">Nordic Council</a> in their own right as part of Denmark's membership. Although the Kingdom of Denmark is a member of the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>, both areas have special dispensation and remain outside the EU. Greenland joined the EU as part of Denmark in 1973, but <a href="/wiki/1982_Greenlandic_European_Economic_Community_membership_referendum" class="mw-redirect" title="1982 Greenlandic European Economic Community membership referendum">opted to leave</a> in 1985 after Greenlandic home rule was introduced in 1979.
</p><p>The "Home Rule Act of the Faroe Islands" specifies that a 'Faroese' shall be understood to mean a person who is a "<i>national</i> of Denmark and a <i>resident</i> of the Faroe Islands".<sup id="cite_ref-Faroese_home_rule_8-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Faroese_home_rule-8">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> The Government of Denmark issues special passports for its citizens living in the Faroe Islands and Greenland with the right to choose a regular <a href="/wiki/Danish_passport" title="Danish passport">Danish passport</a> as well. The Faroese Home Rule Act states that, in <a href="/wiki/Faroese_passport" class="mw-redirect" title="Faroese passport">Faroese passports</a>, <i>Føroyingur</i> (Faroese) and <i>Føroyar</i> (Faroe Islands) shall be inserted after the words <i>Dansk</i> (Danish) and <i>Danmark</i> (Denmark).<sup id="cite_ref-Faroese_home_rule_8-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Faroese_home_rule-8">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Not_devolved">Not devolved</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Danish_Realm&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Not devolved">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The provisions for home rule are limited to internal matters only. Neither Greenland nor the Faroe Islands can write laws that concern the relationship with other states, nor laws that apply to the entire Realm; furthermore, the Supreme Court (Danish: <i>Højesteret</i>) in Copenhagen is the final legal instance, and legal matters from Greenland and the Faroe Islands must be prepared for that court, like any Danish matter. Danish currency is also legal tender in Greenland, but not in the Faroes.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> Denmark is responsible for the military defence of both nations.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Relationship_with_the_European_Union">Relationship with the European Union</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Danish_Realm&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Relationship with the European Union">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The Kingdom of Denmark is a member state of the <a href="/wiki/European_Communities" title="European Communities">European Communities</a>, the predecessor of the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>, since 1973. In 1982, <a href="/wiki/Greenland" title="Greenland">Greenland</a> <a href="/wiki/1982_Greenlandic_European_Communities_membership_referendum" title="1982 Greenlandic European Communities membership referendum">voted to leave the Communities</a> after gaining <a href="/wiki/Home_rule" title="Home rule">home rule</a> from the Realm of Denmark.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Faroe_Islands" title="Faroe Islands">Faroe Islands</a> was never part of the EU, as explicitly asserted by both Rome treaties.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> The relations of the Faroe Islands with the EU are governed by a Fisheries Agreement (1977) and a <a href="/wiki/Free_Trade_Agreement" class="mw-redirect" title="Free Trade Agreement">Free Trade Agreement</a> (1991, revised 1998). The main reason for remaining outside the EU is disagreements about the <a href="/wiki/Common_Fisheries_Policy" title="Common Fisheries Policy">Common Fisheries Policy</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Terminology">Terminology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Danish_Realm&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Terminology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<dl><dt><span title="Danish-language text"><i lang="da">Hjemmestyre</i></span></dt>
<dd>Meaning "<a href="/wiki/Home_rule" title="Home rule">home rule</a>", it indicates an autonomous administration (present in both in Greenland and the Faroe Islands) that has power over many internal affairs. In this arrangement, the Danish government deals with external matters such as defence and foreign affairs. Greenland and the Faroe Islands maintain their own elected assemblies and administrations, headed by a premier who appoints a cabinet. This is synonymous with "self-governing".</dd></dl>
<dl><dt><span title="Danish-language text"><i lang="da">Selvstyre</i></span></dt>
<dd>Following <a href="/wiki/2008_Greenlandic_self-government_referendum" title="2008 Greenlandic self-government referendum">a referendum</a> on 25 November 2008 (the 30th anniversary of the establishment of home rule in Greenland), the relationship between the Danish and Greenlandic governments changed, with Greenland gaining greater autonomy. Further powers were granted to the Greenlandic government on 21 June 2009, including control of the police force, coastguard, and courts. Additionally, Greenland now receives fewer Danish subsidies, becoming more self-sufficient.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> As a result of these changes, Greenland was then said to have <i>self rule</i> with minimal support from Denmark, as opposed to "home rule".</dd></dl>
<dl><dt><span title="Danish-language text"><i lang="da">Rigsombudsmand</i></span></dt>
<dd><a href="/wiki/High_Commissioners" class="mw-redirect" title="High Commissioners">High Commissioners</a> represent the interests of Denmark in the <a href="/wiki/High_Commission_of_Denmark_in_the_Faroe_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="High Commission of Denmark in the Faroe Islands">Faroe Islands</a> and <a href="/wiki/High_Commission_of_Denmark_in_Greenland" class="mw-redirect" title="High Commission of Denmark in Greenland">Greenland</a>. There is one Danish High Commissioner in each territory. The commissioner can attend the meetings at the Løgting in the Faroes and at the Inatsisartut in Greenland, but can not vote.</dd></dl>
<dl><dt><span title="Danish-language text"><i lang="da">Folketingsmedlemmer fra Færøerne og Grønland</i></span></dt>
<dd>Members of the Folketing from the Faroe Islands and Greenland: Greenland and the Faroe Islands and their self-rule administrations take part in consultations on policies and decisions affecting their region, including negotiations with the devolved legislatures and the <a href="/wiki/Folketing" title="Folketing">Danish parliament (folketing)</a>. Greenland and the Faroe Islands have two members of the Danish <span title="Danish-language text"><i lang="da">folketing</i></span> each, with full voting privileges.</dd></dl>

<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-2"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-2">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><i>Kong Christian</i> has equal status as a <a href="/wiki/National_anthem" title="National anthem">national anthem</a> but is generally used only on <a href="/wiki/Honors_music" class="mw-redirect" title="Honors music">royal</a> and military occasions.<sup id="cite_ref-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-central_cities-3"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-central_cities_3-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Copenhagen is the constitutional capital of the kingdom and <a href="/wiki/Denmark" title="Denmark">Denmark</a>; <a href="/wiki/T%C3%B3rshavn" title="Tórshavn">Tórshavn</a> is the capital of the <a href="/wiki/Faroe_Islands" title="Faroe Islands">Faroe Islands</a>; <a href="/wiki/Nuuk" title="Nuuk">Nuuk</a> is the capital of <a href="/wiki/Greenland" title="Greenland">Greenland</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-6"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-6">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Faroese is co-official with Danish in the Faroe Islands.<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> Greenlandic is the sole official language in Greenland.<sup id="cite_ref-Sproglov_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sproglov-5">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> German is recognised as a protected minority language in the South Jutland area of Denmark.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-currency-16"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-currency_16-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">In the Faroe Islands, the currency has a separate design and is known as the <a href="/wiki/Faroese_kr%C3%B3na" title="Faroese króna">Faroese króna</a>, but is not a separate currency.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-18"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-18">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Also <a href="/wiki/.eu" title=".eu">.eu</a>, shared with other <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">EU</a> member states.</span>
</li>

<li id="cite_note-67"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-67">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.stm.dk/_p_13090.html">Description of the Greenlandic Self-Government Act on the webpage of the Ministry of State of Denmark</a>"The Self-Government Act provides for the Self-Government authorities to assume a number of new fields of responsibility, such as administration of justice, including the establishment of courts of law; the prison and probation service; the police; the field relating to company law, accounting and auditing; mineral resource activities; aviation; law of legal capacity, family law and succession law; aliens and border controls; the working environment; as well as <a href="/wiki/Financial_regulation" title="Financial regulation">financial regulation</a> and supervision, cf. Schedule I and II in the Annex to the Self-Government Act."</span>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="General">General</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Danish_Realm&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: General">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.stm.dk/_p_10988.html"><i>The unity of the Realm</i>; the status the Faroe Islands and Greenland</a> (Prime Minister's Office)</li>
